\Pi_{I}(t_{1},t_{2})
is the
\xi
propagator.

These integrals have two key differences compared to the perturbative sector. First, the propagator depends on
t_{1}
and
t_{2}

separately
, not just on
t_{1}-t_{2}
, because the instanton breaks time-translation invariance. Second, the cubic vertex depends on the instanton background
x_{I}(t)
. These complications make the loops rather challenging. However, once the perturbative contributions to the same graphs are subtracted, the diagrams are all finite and can be computed numerically if needed.

A fourth diagram exists at 2 loops in the instanton sector that has no perturbative analog. The change of variables to the collective coordinate
t_{0}
(the instanton position) introduces a Jacobian whose expansion in the transverse fluctuation
\xi(t)=x(t+t_{0})-x_{I}(t)
has a linear piece
-(1/S_{I})\!\int\!dt\,\ddot{x}_{I}\xi
, which acts as a tadpole-source vertex. Although a true
action
tadpole is forbidden by the equation of motion
\ddot{x}_{I}=V^{\prime}(x_{I})
at the saddle, the source from the measure does not vanish: it leaves an additional 2-loop topology with the source contracting through one propagator into a self-loop on a cubic vertex,

G_{1}=\vbox{\hbox{\hbox to42.01pt{\vbox to22.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 16.42638pt\lower-11.35846pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -19.69 0 M -17.19 0 C -17.19 1.38 -18.31 2.49 -19.69 2.49 C -21.06 2.49 -22.18 1.38 -22.18 0 C -22.18 -1.38 -21.06 -2.49 -19.69 -2.49 C -18.31 -2.49 -17.19 -1.38 -17.19 0 Z M -19.69 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -19.69 0 L 5.91 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\pgfinnerlinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -19.69 0 L 5.91 0}{fill:none} }\lxSVG@closescope \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 0 M 8.4 0 C 8.4 1.38 7.28 2.49 5.91 2.49 C 4.53 2.49 3.41 1.38 3.41 0 C 3.41 -1.38 4.53 -2.49 5.91 -2.49 C 7.28 -2.49 8.4 -1.38 8.4 0 Z M 5.91 0}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 M 33.46 0 C 33.46 7.61 27.3 13.78 19.69 13.78 C 12.07 13.78 5.91 7.61 5.91 0 C 5.91 -7.61 12.07 -13.78 19.69 -13.78 C 27.3 -13.78 33.46 -7.61 33.46 0 Z M 19.69 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\pgfinnerlinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 19.69 0 M 33.46 0 C 33.46 7.61 27.3 13.78 19.69 13.78 C 12.07 13.78 5.91 7.61 5.91 0 C 5.91 -7.61 12.07 -13.78 19.69 -13.78 C 27.3 -13.78 33.46 -7.61 33.46 0 Z M 19.69 0}{fill:none} }\lxSVG@closescope \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}=\frac{9\hbar}{4}\int dt_{1}\,dt_{2}\,x_{I}(t_{1})\,\ddot{x}_{I}(t_{2})\,\Pi_{I}(t_{1},t_{1})\Pi_{I}(t_{1},t_{2})\,.

(5.168)

This diagram is derived in Appendix
E
and gives a critical contribution (without it the shift in the energies would not agree with Exact WKB; see
Section
5.9
).

The result of the computation to 3 loops, at leading order in the
T\to\infty
limit, is

\Delta_{L}(\hbar)\;=\;-\frac{71}{48}\,\hbar\;-\;\frac{315}{128}\,\hbar^{2}\;+\;\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{3})\,.

(5.169)

This is the single-instanton result. Around an
n
-instanton background, the result gets multiplied by
n
since the instantons do not interact at infinite
T
. Finite-
T
corrections to
\Delta_{L}
require using the exact-instanton (Lamé) propagator rather than its
T\to\infty
approximation (Pöschl–Teller). The exact propagator can likely be computed in closed form using the Lamé spectrum, but the resulting expression would be a complicated elliptic function of
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
and the time integrals would be formidable. These finite-
T
corrections would be needed only at the level of precision required to extract
N
-dependent corrections to the non-perturbative parts of the excited-state energies. On the Exact WKB side, the analogous integrals are those of the quantum action
S_{N}^{m}
, which can be done algebraically using the closure of the Picard–Fuchs equation. Thus from a practical point of view it does not seem worthwhile to attempt the finite-
T
calculation on the path integral side.

5.5
n=2
thimble decomposition

We now have almost all the ingredients in Eq. (
5.106
): the action, the transverse fluctuation determinant with the quasi-zero modes removed, the perturbative vacuum-bubble factor
\Delta_{V}
, and the instanton-background loop correction
\Delta_{L}
. It remains to integrate over the quasi-zero modes. As a reminder, the full integration contour is decomposed as a sum over Lefschetz thimbles passing through saddles (Section
5.3.2
), and only the real instanton saddles contribute in the decomposition (see Section
5.3.4
). The thimbles factorize into transverse and quasi-collective-coordinate pieces as described around Eq. (
5.105
), and we are now interested in computing the finite-dimensional quasi-collective-coordinate integrals. These integrals are along complex thimbles and are in general complex. We begin here with the
n=2
case, which is already very intricate. Our goal is to understand the origin of the imaginary parts from the geometry of the quasi-zero mode space and to see how they cancel with the Borel resummation of the
n=0
sector asymptotic series.

Concretely, the program of this section is:

1.

Establish the quasi-zero-mode geometry around the
n=2
saddle: the collective coordinate
\alpha
for the instanton separation, the QZM Jacobian, and the complex saddle structure in the
\alpha
-plane.

2.

Compute the thimble integral
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}
through the real
n=2
saddle and identify the imaginary part which contributes to
Z_{2}
.

3.

Extract the leading-order asymptotic series of
Z_{0}
from the integral over real
\alpha
directions between the
n=0
and
n=2
saddles.

4.

Assemble the resurgent structure, where the lateral Borel resummation of
Z_{0}
produces an imaginary ambiguity that exactly cancels the explicit imaginary part of
Z_{2}
.

Following sections treating the
n>2
decomposition will follow a similar road.

5.5.1
Quasi-zero-mode geometry for
n=2

We begin the
n=2
analysis with the effective action from Eq. (
5.84
). Recall that this action is computed by approximating the instanton solutions by sewing together tanh functions, evaluating the action, and then rescaling the exponentially suppressed terms to agree with the action of the exact saddle at large
T
. The resulting effective action
S_{\mathrm{eff}}
captures the leading large-
T
QZM dynamics and matches the first exponentially small correction to the exact saddle action. We can check numerically that the exact action agrees with
S_{\mathrm{eff}}
along the QZM direction near the saddle (see Fig.
15
). With
\alpha_{1}=\alpha
and
\alpha_{2}=T-\alpha
and
n=2k=2
the effective action is

S_{\mathrm{eff}}(\alpha)=2S_{I}-12S_{I}\left[e^{-\alpha}+e^{-(T-\alpha)}\right]\,.

(5.170)

Following the convention of Eq. (
5.277
), we subtract the saddle action and define

S_{\text{eff}}^{{(2)}}(\alpha)\equiv S_{\text{eff}}(\alpha)-2S_{I}=-12S_{I}\!\left[e^{-\alpha}+e^{-(T-\alpha)}\right]\,.

(5.171)

Here
\alpha
is the distance between the instantons, so its natural range is
0\leq\operatorname{Re}\alpha\leq T
. The standard interpretation of this action is that
e^{-\alpha}
and
e^{-T+\alpha}
represent interactions between instantons on a circle of circumference
T
. The action is invariant under
\alpha\to\alpha+2\pi i
and we therefore take
-\pi\leq\operatorname{Im}\alpha\leq\pi
. There are then two extrema of this action in the fundamental domain, at
\alpha=T/2
and
\alpha=T/2+i\pi
. These correspond to the real instanton
x_{1,0}
and the complex instanton
x_{1,1}
shown in Fig.
12
on the
x_{2}
branch.

Figure 15:
Starting from the exact
n=2
saddle at finite
T
, we follow gradient flow to the
n=0
saddle at
x=-1
. Left: action along the flow, plotted as
S(\alpha)
with the normalization
\alpha(u{=}0)=T/2
and
\alpha(u_{\rm end})=0
. Right: representative profiles
x(t)
along the same trajectory, labeled by
\alpha
. The overall normalization of the flow parameter
u
is arbitrary.

To integrate over
\alpha
we need to establish the normalization of the associated mode. Around the (1,0) saddle there is one exact zero mode parameterized by a global time translation parameter
t_{0}
and a quasi-zero mode parameterized by
\alpha
. We would like to parametrize this 2D space close to the saddle as

x(t)=x_{1,0}(t)+c_{0}\,\Psi_{0}(t)+c_{1}\,\Psi_{1}(t),

(5.172)

where
\Psi_{0}
and
\Psi_{1}
are respectively the normalized zero mode and unstable mode of the exact Hessian. We need to go from the integration measure over
t_{0}
and
\alpha
to the orthonormal coordinates
c_{0}
and
c_{1}
. We start with two instantons with centers at
t_{1},t_{2}
. When the instantons are far separated, all the quasi-zero modes are associated with the independent motion of each instanton center. So at leading order we can use
\|\dot{x}_{a}\|^{2}=S_{I}
, giving a Jacobian factor
\sqrt{S_{I}/(2\pi\hbar)}
for each mode in
t_{1},t_{2}
coordinate space. For the
n=2
case we are considering here,
t_{0}=(t_{1}+t_{2})/2
is the global translation coordinate and
\alpha=t_{1}-t_{2}
is the difference between the instanton centers. So we have

dc_{0}^{2}+dc_{1}^{2}=S_{I}(dt_{1}^{2}+dt_{2}^{2})=2S_{I}\,dt_{0}^{2}+\frac{S_{I}}{2}\,d\alpha^{2}.

(5.173)

Thus the canonically normalized coordinates are related by
c_{0}=\sqrt{2S_{I}}t_{0}
and

\alpha=\frac{T}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{2}{S_{I}}}\,c_{1}+{\cal O}(c_{1}^{2})\,.

(5.174)

So in particular, the Jacobian associated with
d\alpha
differs by a factor of
1/\sqrt{2}
from that of an individual center coordinate. As a check, we can expand Eq. (
5.170
) about
\alpha=T/2
:

S\left(\frac{T}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{2}{S_{I}}}\,c_{1}\right)=2S_{I}-24S_{I}e^{-\frac{T}{2}}-24e^{-\frac{T}{2}}c_{1}^{2}+\cdots

(5.175)

So the Hessian entry
d^{2}S/dc_{1}^{2}
is
-48e^{-T/2}
, in exact agreement with Eq. (
5.142
) for
n=2,\;m=1
.

\operatorname{Im}\hbar>0

\operatorname{Im}\hbar<0

Figure 16:
Lefschetz thimbles using the
n=2
effective action
S_{\text{eff}}
from Eq. (
5.170
) with
\hbar\to\hbar e^{i\varepsilon}
. The perturbative saddles at
x=\pm 1
are plotted at
\alpha=0,T
and shown as the orange dots, with their thimbles in orange. The
(1,0)
real saddle is the black dot at
\alpha=\frac{T}{2}
. Its thimble goes off in the imaginary direction then veers real at the complex Stokes point associated with the
(1,1)
saddle. The green thimble
\mathcal{J}_{1,1}
through the (1,1) saddle maintains an imaginary offset
\operatorname{Im}\alpha=\pi
. The imaginary direction is compact, so the
\mathcal{J}_{1,1}
thimble shown with
\operatorname{Im}\alpha=\pi
is the same as the one with
\operatorname{Im}\alpha=-\pi
.

Since
\alpha
is the quasi-collective coordinate associated with a quasi-zero mode with eigenvalue
\sim e^{-T/2}
, we cannot integrate over it in the Gaussian approximation, and instead integrate over its full thimble. The thimbles for
S_{\text{eff}}
are shown in Fig.
16
. For these plots we deformed
\hbar\to\hbar e^{i\varepsilon}
to resolve the Stokes ambiguity. The real part of the action for the (1,0) saddle increases in the imaginary
\alpha
direction until it hits the (1,1) saddle. Note that the (1,1) saddle is mirrored at
\pm i\pi
due to the invariance of the action under
\alpha\to\alpha+2\pi i
. The saddles at
x=\pm 1
are not extrema of
S_{\text{eff}}
since the effective action is only valid at large
T
. However, they live in the directions going towards
\alpha=0
and
\alpha=T
. These boundary points are indicated in the figure as orange dots, with associated thimbles starting on the real axis, proceeding to the
(1,0)
Stokes point, before following along the
\mathcal{J}_{1,0}
trajectory in the complex plane.

Although
\alpha
is only well defined when the instantons are well separated, there is still a unique steepest descent trajectory descending from the (1,0) saddle. We can find this trajectory numerically. The exact action
S[x]=\int_{0}^{T}dt\,[\tfrac{1}{2}\dot{x}^{2}-U(x)]
is a functional of the path
x(t)
. Starting from a path slightly perturbed from
x_{1,0}
in the direction of the unstable mode, we evolve under gradient flow,
\partial_{u}x(t)=-\delta S/\delta x(t)
, computing the action integral on the discretized path at each flow step. The result is shown in Fig.
15
where the exact action along the flow is compared to
S_{\text{eff}}
, both agreeing in the large-
\alpha
regime near the
n=2
saddle. The flow trajectory interpolates between the real
n=2
instanton saddle and the static
n=0
path at
x=-1
.

5.5.2
n=2
thimble integral

We now move to compute the thimble integrals in the complexified
\alpha
space. We begin with the
n=2
thimble integral through
\mathcal{J}_{1,0}^{\pm}
, where the superscript refers to the sign of
\operatorname{Im}\hbar
as in Fig.
16
, used to resolve the Stokes ambiguity. This integral can be decomposed into two equal horizontal arms coming in from infinity to the complex saddles, and two equal vertical arms, going between the complex saddles and the real ones:

\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}(T)=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{1,0}^{\pm}}d\alpha\,e^{-S_{\text{eff}}^{(2)}/\hbar}=2I^{\text{arm}}(T)\pm 2I^{\text{vert}}(T)

(5.176)

The arm integral is

\displaystyle I^{\text{arm}}(T)

\displaystyle=\int_{i\pi-\infty}^{i\pi}d\alpha\;e^{-S_{\text{eff}}^{(2)}/\hbar}=\int_{-\infty}^{0}dx\;\exp\!\left[-\frac{24S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/2}\cosh x\right]

(5.177)

\displaystyle=K_{0}\!\left(\frac{24S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/2}\right),

where we parameterized
\alpha=T/2+i\pi+x
and used
\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-a\cosh x}dx=K_{0}(a)
. For large
T
,

I^{\text{arm}}(T)=\frac{T}{2}-\gamma_{E}-\log\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)+\mathcal{O}\!\left(Te^{-T}\right)\,.

(5.178)

The vertical integral over the imaginary contour between the two saddles gives

\displaystyle I^{\text{vert}}(T)

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{T}{2}+i\pi}^{\frac{T}{2}}d\alpha\;e^{-S_{\text{eff}}^{(2)}/\hbar}=-i\,\int_{0}^{\pi}dx\;\exp\!\left[-\frac{24S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/2}\cos x\right]

(5.179)

\displaystyle=-i\pi\,I_{0}\!\left(\frac{24S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/2}\right),

where now we used
\alpha=T/2+i\pi-ix
and
\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{-a\cos x}dx=\pi I_{0}(a)
.
For large
T
,

I^{\text{vert}}(T)=-i\pi\left[1+\mathcal{O}\!\left(e^{-T}\right)\right].

(5.180)

Adding these as in Eq. (
5.176
) gives

\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}(T)=2\left[\frac{T}{2}-\gamma_{E}-\log\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)\mp\pi i\right]+\mathcal{O}(Te^{-T}).

(5.181)

An alternative way to compute
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}(T)
is directly along the thimble without decomposing it into parts. This can be done by continuing
\hbar\to e^{i\pi}\hbar
so that the thimble aligns with
\mathbb{R}
, and then we can use

\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\mathbb{R}}(T)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\alpha\,e^{S_{\text{eff}}^{(2)}/\hbar}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\alpha\exp\left[-\frac{24S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/2}\cosh(\alpha-T/2)\right]=\mathcal{G}_{2}(\kappa_{2}^{2})

(5.182)

where
\mathcal{G}_{n}
is the all-zero-parameter Meijer
G
-function (the inverse Mellin transform of
\Gamma(s)^{n}
):

\mathcal{G}_{n}(z)\equiv G_{0,n}^{n,0}\!\left(z\,\big|\,\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{n}\right)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int^{c+i\infty}_{c-i\infty}ds\,\Gamma(s)^{n}z^{-s},\qquad c>0,

(5.183)

with

\kappa_{2}=\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/2}\,.

(5.184)

Then,

\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}(T)=\mathcal{I}^{\mathbb{R}}_{2}(\kappa_{2}e^{\pm i\pi})=\mathcal{G}_{2}(\kappa_{2}^{2}e^{\pm 2\pi i})=2\,K_{0}(-2\kappa_{2}\pm i\varepsilon)\,.

(5.185)

At large
T
, small
\kappa_{2}

\mathcal{G}_{2}(\kappa_{2}^{2})=-\ln\kappa_{2}^{2}-2\gamma_{E}\,,

(5.186)

which when combined with Eqs. (
5.185
) and (
5.184
) agrees with Eq. (
5.181
). This method will be more practical for the higher instanton sectors.

The integral
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}
over the
n=2
thimble is a multiplicative factor in the
n=2
sector of the partition function trans-series, as in Eq. (
5.106
). Recall that we write
Z=\sum_{n}\lambda^{n}Z_{n}(T,\hbar)
where
\lambda=e^{-S_{I}/\hbar}
. The transverse determinant factor is
(\det_{\perp}\mathcal{O}_{2})^{-1/2}=Z_{\text{SHO}}\cdot(\det\mathcal{O}_{0}/\det_{\perp}\mathcal{O}_{2})^{1/2}
. The determinant ratio with the zero mode and quasi-zero mode removed is given in (
5.145
) at large
T
:
(\det\mathcal{O}_{0}/\det_{\perp}\mathcal{O}_{2})^{1/2}=12
. The collective-coordinate Jacobian from Eq. (
5.173
) has
\sqrt{S_{I}/\pi\hbar}
from the zero mode and
\sqrt{S_{I}/4\pi\hbar}
from the quasi-collective coordinate, contributing
S_{I}/(2\pi\hbar)
to the path-integral measure. The two wells contribute a factor of 2, which cancels against the correction factor from the change to collective coordinate, which is
1/n=1/2
, where
n
is the number of times the origin is crossed. Combining
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{+}
from Eq. (
5.181
) with these factors,

\displaystyle Z_{2}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=Z_{\text{SHO}}\!\left(\frac{\det{\mathcal{O}}_{0}}{\det_{\perp}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}}\right)^{\!1/2}\frac{S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\,\int_{0}^{T}dt_{0}\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}

(5.187)

\displaystyle=Z_{\text{SHO}}\frac{6S_{I}T}{\pi\hbar}\left[T-2\gamma_{E}-2\log\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)\mp 2\pi i\right].

(5.188)

This is a complex result, with the imaginary part coming from
2I^{\text{vert}}=-2\pi i
. Its imaginary part is

\operatorname{Im}Z_{2}^{\pm}=\mp 2\pi Z_{\text{SHO}}\frac{6S_{I}T}{\pi\hbar}\,.

(5.189)

This is qualitatively and quantitatively different from the dilute-gas approximation which associates the
\lambda^{2}
terms effectively with an integral along the real
\alpha
direction. In the dilute gas, the equivalent of
Z_{2}^{\pm}
is real.

5.5.3
n=0
asymptotic series

Next we consider the implications of
S_{\text{eff}}
for the perturbative series. The leading asymptotic growth of the trans-series in the
n=0
sector is determined by the nearest saddle, which is the one at
n=2
, and can be reproduced by integrating along the valley direction
\alpha
between the two saddle points. This integral is a little more subtle because, as shown in Fig.
15
, we cannot trust
S_{\text{eff}}
away from the
\alpha=\frac{T}{2}
region. However, because the asymptotic growth comes from the existence of the
n=2
saddle, the detailed structure of the action in the
\alpha\approx 0
region is not critical: a change in
S_{\text{eff}}
in this region will only give finite corrections to the terms in the series which can be included as matching corrections to a perturbative calculation with the full action. We will extract the asymptotic series from the integral along
\alpha
between the
n=0
saddle, represented by an endpoint at
\alpha=\alpha_{0}
up to the
n=2
saddle at
\alpha=T/2
. The value of
\alpha_{0}
can be fixed by location of the Borel pole at
t=2S_{I}
, or equivalently that the action gap is
S_{\text{eff}}(T/2)-S_{\text{eff}}(\alpha_{0})=2S_{I}
, which gives
\alpha_{0}=\ln 6
.

The real integral along
\alpha
between the saddles that gives the asymptotic growth is then

I_{0\to 2}(T)=\int_{\alpha_{0}}^{\frac{T}{2}}d\alpha\,e^{-\frac{1}{\hbar}S_{\text{eff}}}=e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{\alpha_{0}}^{T/2}d\alpha\,\exp\!\left[+\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\left(e^{-\alpha}+e^{-(T-\alpha)}\right)\right]\,.

(5.190)

Since
\alpha<T/2
we can use
\exp\!\left[\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-(T-\alpha)}\right]=1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-T/2})
uniformly on the full integration range. Therefore,

\displaystyle I_{0\to 2}(T)

\displaystyle=e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{\alpha_{0}}^{T/2}d\alpha\,\exp\!\left[\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-\alpha}\right]+\mathcal{O}\!\left(Te^{-T/2}\right)

(5.191)

\displaystyle=e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{\kappa_{2}}^{u_{0}}\frac{du}{u}\,e^{u}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(Te^{-T/2}\right),

(5.192)

where
u\equiv\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-\alpha}
with
u_{0}=\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-\alpha_{0}}
. This integral can be done using
\int_{a}^{b}du\,e^{u}/u=\operatorname{Ein}(-a)-\operatorname{Ein}(-b)+\ln(b/a)
, where

\operatorname{Ein}(z)\equiv\int_{0}^{z}dt\,\frac{1-e^{-t}}{t}\,.

(5.193)

Then

I_{0\to 2}(T)=e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\left[\operatorname{Ein}(-\kappa_{2})-\operatorname{Ein}(-u_{0})+\ln\frac{u_{0}}{\kappa_{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}\!\left(Te^{-T/2}\right)\,,

(5.194)

The function
\operatorname{Ein}(z)
is an entire function on the complex plane, and
\operatorname{Ein}(z)
is real for real
z
. For positive real
z
, its large-
z
expansion on the negative axis is the real formal trans-series

\operatorname{Ein}(-z)\sim\gamma_{E}+\ln(-z)-e^{z}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{n!}{z^{n+1}}\,,

(5.195)

Since
\kappa_{2}\sim e^{-T/2}\to 0
as
T\to\infty
, the
\operatorname{Ein}(-\kappa_{2})
contribution to Eq. (
5.194
) vanishes in this limit (as can be seen from the
z\to 0
limit of Eq. (
5.193
)) and we can drop it. We cannot drop the
\kappa_{2}
in the logarithm in Eq. (
5.194
) though since it is linear in
T
. Inserting the trans-series expansion of
\operatorname{Ein}
we then get

\displaystyle I_{0\to 2}^{\text{asy}}(T)

\displaystyle\sim e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\left[-\operatorname{Ein}(-u_{0})+\ln\frac{u_{0}}{\kappa_{2}}\right]

(5.196)

\displaystyle=e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\left[\exp\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-\alpha_{0}}\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n!\left(\frac{\hbar\,e^{\alpha_{0}}}{12S_{I}}\right)^{n+1}+\frac{T}{2}-\gamma_{E}-\ln\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)\pm i\pi\right]\,.

(5.197)

The
\alpha_{0}
factors are obviously awkward, and we will remove them shortly with
\alpha_{0}=\ln 6
. However, before doing so, we observe critically that the imaginary ambiguity of the lateral Borel resummation of the perturbative series can be read directly from the factorial tail in Eq. (
5.195
):

\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}\!\left[\exp\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-\alpha_{0}}\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n!\left(\frac{\hbar\,e^{\alpha_{0}}}{12S_{I}}\right)^{n+1}\right]=\pm\pi\,.

(5.198)

Therefore, the imaginary ambiguity from the resummation of the perturbative series only is

\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}\big[I_{0\to 2}^{\text{asy}}(T)\big]=\pm\pi e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\,,

(5.199)

independent of
\alpha_{0}
. Naturally, since
I_{0\to 2}
is real by definition, within that integral the resummation ambiguity cancels against the explicit
\pm i\pi
factor. However, for a quantity that involves only the perturbative series without the explicit additional
\pm i\pi
, like the thimble integrals
\mathcal{J}_{\pm 1}
, or the
n=0
asymptotic series, the resulting imaginary ambiguity is not canceled. Its magnitude is exactly half of the imaginary part of
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}
in Eq. (
5.181
) as expected from the geometry, and is independent of the boundary value chosen for
\alpha_{0}
. That being said, the real part of the trans-series does depend on
\alpha_{0}
, and since the
n=0
sector cannot be suppressed by any power of
e^{-S_{I}/\hbar}
, we are led to the unique choice
\alpha_{0}=\ln 6
. This choice corresponds to
S_{\text{eff}}(\alpha_{0})=0
, so that the action difference between the boundaries is
S_{\text{eff}}(T/2)-S_{\text{eff}}(\alpha_{0})=2S_{I}
. With this choice, the asymptotic series becomes

I_{0\to 2}^{\text{asy}}(T)\sim\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{n+1}+\mathcal{O}(e^{-T/2})\,.

(5.200)

Note that terms suppressed by
e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}
are non-perturbative and not part of the formal asymptotic series expansion. With
\alpha_{0}=\ln 6
, so
u_{0}=2S_{I}/\hbar
, the full integral from
Eq. (
5.194
) becomes in the large-
T
limit

I_{0\to 2}(T)=e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\left[-\operatorname{Ein}\!\left(-\frac{2S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)+\frac{T}{2}-\ln 6+\mathcal{O}\!\left(T\,e^{-T/2}\right)\right]\,.

(5.201)

The asymptotic expansion of
I_{0\to 2}(T)
using Eq. (
5.195
) agrees with
I_{0\to 2}^{\text{asy}}(T)
.

The full thimble integral through the perturbative saddle is then the sum of the
0\to 2
segment, one vertical segment with opposite orientation, and one arm with opposite orientation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{\pm 1}}d\alpha\,e^{-S_{\text{eff}}/\hbar}=I_{0\to 2}(T)-I^{\text{arm}}(T)\mp I^{\text{vert}}(T)

(5.202)

\displaystyle=-e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}E_{1}\!\left(-\frac{2S_{I}}{\hbar}\pm i\varepsilon\right)\,,

(5.203)

which is indeed the lateral Borel resummation of Eq. (
5.200
). Here we have expressed the answer in terms of another exponential integral function

E_{1}(z\pm i\varepsilon)=\Gamma(0,z\pm i\varepsilon)=\int_{z\pm i\varepsilon}^{\infty}dt\,\frac{e^{-t}}{t}\,,

(5.204)

where
E_{1}
is related to
Ein
by

\operatorname{Ein}(z)=E_{1}(z\pm i\varepsilon)+\ln(z\,e^{\gamma_{E}}\pm i\varepsilon)\,.

(5.205)

Unlike Ein, which is entire,
E_{1}(z)
has a logarithmic branch cut for
z<0
.

With all the relevant thimble integrals in hand for the Picard–Lefschetz decomposition of the real
\alpha
contour, we can combine them together. The range of that original integral is
0\leq\operatorname{Re}\alpha\leq T
, which gets decomposed as

\int_{0}^{T}d\alpha\,e^{-S_{\text{eff}}/\hbar}=\left[\int_{\mathcal{J}_{-1}}+\int_{\mathcal{J}_{1,0}}+\int_{\mathcal{J}_{1}}\right]d\alpha\,e^{-S_{\text{eff}}/\hbar}=e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\left[-2\operatorname{Ein}\!\left(-\frac{2S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)+T-2\ln 6\right]\,.

(5.206)

This sum is manifestly real as expected. Indeed, this is nothing but
2I_{0\to 2}(T)
, as can be seen from the geometry. The
\ln 6
is an artifact of using
S_{\text{eff}}
away from the region near the
n=2
saddle where it is derived, and is subleading at large
T
; we only include it for completeness.

5.5.4
Resurgent structure at order
\lambda^{2}

Next, we construct the trans-series for
Z_{0}
and
Z_{2}
, then convert to the energies using Eq. (
5.9
). We will verify the cancellation of the Borel ambiguity at order
\lambda^{2}
. We will focus on the ground state energy only in this section and leave the discussion of the full spectrum to Section
5.9
.

First, we consider the
Z_{0}
series. This represents the perturbative expansion around the perturbative saddles at
x=\pm 1
. More precisely, it is the sum of the two expansions, since both the intersection numbers are
\eta_{\pm}=1
. The saddle point approximation around each individual saddle was computed with Feynman diagrams in Section
5.4.4
. Keeping the leading large-
T
vacuum-bubble correction from Eq. (
5.163
),

Z_{0}=2\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\,\exp\!\left[T\!\left(\frac{\hbar}{4}+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2})\right)\right]=2\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\left(1+\frac{T}{4}\hbar+\cdots\right)\,.

(5.207)

The exponent represents the sum of connected graphs, each of which has a factor of
T
from time-translation invariance. Higher order terms are in principle computable from Feynman diagrams as in Eq. (
5.163
), although the diagrammatic approach is not very efficient, as compared to say, Bender–Wu (see Appendix
A
).

The leading asymptotic behavior of the series in
Z_{0}
is determined by the path integral in the direction towards the nearest Stokes point, at the
n=2
instanton saddle. The relevant integral is
I_{0\to 2}^{\text{asy}}
in the space of quasi-zero-modes around the
n=2
saddle. Its asymptotic expansion is given in Eq. (
5.200
). The coordinates used for this integral involve the zero mode
t_{0}
for the instanton-pair center and the quasi-zero mode
\alpha
for the instanton-pair separation. The remaining modes are spectators to the asymptotic growth and can be treated as Gaussian. Thus the prefactor is the same as in Eq. (
5.187
) and we have

Z_{0}\sim 2Z_{\text{SHO}}\!\left(\frac{\det{\mathcal{O}}_{0}}{\det_{\perp}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}}\right)^{\!1/2}\frac{S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\,\int_{0}^{T}dt_{0}\,\mathcal{I}_{0\to 2}^{\text{asy}}

(5.208)

where the overall factor of 2 comes from summing the series around the two perturbative saddles. Plugging in the factors as in Eqs. (
5.188
) and (
5.200
) and combining with the leading perturbative vacuum-bubble correction gives

Z_{0}=2\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\left(1+\frac{T}{4}\hbar+\cdots+T\frac{6S_{I}}{\pi\hbar}\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{n+1}n!\right)\,,

(5.209)

with the
\cdots
terms containing additional analytic
\hbar
dependent functions as well as terms with subleading asymptotic growth (e.g.
(4S_{I})^{-n}n!
).

The lateral Borel resummation of the
Z_{0}
trans-series restores the full integral over the
\mathcal{J}_{\pm 1}
thimbles, as in Eq. (
5.203
). So

\mathcal{S}[Z_{0}]=2Z_{\text{SHO}}\left[1+\frac{T}{4}\hbar-T\frac{6S_{I}}{\pi\hbar}e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}E_{1}\!\left(-\frac{2S_{I}}{\hbar}+i\varepsilon\right)+\cdots\right]\,,

(5.210)

so that using
\operatorname{Im}E_{1}(-g+i\varepsilon)=-\pi
we find

\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}[Z_{0}]=2\pi\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\,T\frac{6S_{I}}{\pi\hbar}e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}+\cdots\,.

(5.211)

Combining with Eq. (
5.189
) we then have for the full partition function

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[Z]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[Z_{0}]+\lambda^{2}\operatorname{Im}Z_{2}^{\pm}+\cdots

(5.212)

\displaystyle=2\pi\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\,T\frac{6S_{I}}{\pi\hbar}e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}-2\pi\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\,T\frac{6S_{I}}{\pi\hbar}e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}=0\,.

(5.213)

The leading Borel ambiguity at order
\lambda^{2}
exactly cancels.

Finally, we translate from the partition function to the energy trans-series using Eq. (
5.9
). Recall from Section
5.1
the convention
E^{(N)}(\hbar)=\sum_{k}\lambda^{k}E_{k}^{(N)}(\hbar)
, where the subscript
k
is the instanton sector and
(N)
labels the energy state (
N=0
ground,
N=1
first excited, …); we drop the state superscript when working with the ground state, so
E_{k}\equiv E_{k}^{(0)}
. At the order needed here, the perturbative energy follows from the leading large-
T
term in Eq. (
5.163
):

E_{0}(\hbar)=\frac{\hbar}{2}-\frac{\hbar^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{3})\,.

(5.214)

Since
Z_{1}=0
in the untwisted trace, the one-instanton splitting is not read off directly; it is either calculated by using the twisted partition function or it appears through
(E_{1})^{2}
in
Z_{2}
. At large
T
, the ground-state doublet dominates
Z_{2}
. Both parity states have the same perturbative energy and the same
(E_{1})^{2}
, while
E_{2}
is parity-independent. Using Eq. (
5.11
), the large-
T
form is

Z_{2}=2\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\left[\frac{T^{2}}{2\hbar^{2}}\bigl(E_{1}\bigr)^{2}-\frac{T}{\hbar}E_{2}\right]\,.

(5.215)

Rewriting the path-integral result (
5.188
) in powers of
T
we have

Z_{2}=2Z_{\text{SHO}}\frac{3S_{I}}{\pi\hbar}\left[T^{2}-2T\!\left(\gamma_{E}+\log\!\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}+\pi i\right)\right]

(5.216)

for the
\operatorname{Im}\hbar>0
lateral prescription. Matching powers of
T
gives the path-integral energy trans-series for the ground-state doublet (
S_{I}=2/3
):

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle=\pm 2\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{\pi}}\,,

(5.217)

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\pi}\left[\gamma_{E}+\ln\frac{8}{\hbar}\pm\pi i\right].

(5.218)

The
\pm
in
E_{1}
distinguishes even and odd parity while the
\pm\pi i
in
E_{2}
reflects the choice of lateral Borel resummation. These results are in exact agreement with what we found with Exact WKB in Section
4
: Eqs. (
4.179
) and (
4.180
) with
\kappa=1/2
for the ground state. In particular, both the one-instanton splitting and two-instanton corrections agree exactly at leading order in
\hbar
and we recover the coefficient of
\ln\hbar
, the imaginary part
\pm\pi i
and the finite real constant
\gamma_{E}
. Interestingly, in the path-integral result
\gamma_{E}
enters through the small-argument expansion
K_{0}(a)\sim-\gamma_{E}-\ln(a/2)
of the arm integral, while in the exact-WKB result it enters as
\psi(1)=-\gamma_{E}
from the Weber equation.

Excited-state energies (
E_{0}^{(2)}
,
E_{0}^{(3)}
, …) are not accessible from the present large-
T
matching, as they contribute to
Z_{2}
only through exponentially suppressed terms
\mathcal{O}(e^{-3T/2})
. For the excited states, we need to keep the full
T
dependence, which can be done in the path integral and is the subject of Section
5.9
.

5.6
n=3
thimble decomposition

The next simplest case is
n=3
. For an odd number of instantons, we need to use the twisted partition function. We use the same convention for the trans-series grading as with
Z
:

\widetilde{Z}=\lambda\,\widetilde{Z}_{1}+\lambda^{3}\,\widetilde{Z}_{3}+\cdots,\qquad\lambda=e^{-S_{I}/\hbar}\,.

(5.219)

The lateral Borel resummation of the asymptotic series in the
n=1
coefficient has an ambiguity of order
\lambda^{2}
and should be canceled by the thimble integral in the
n=3
sector. We will verify this cancellation geometrically and, at leading large
T
, explicitly by computing the relevant parts of the two trans-series sectors.

5.6.1
Quasi-zero-mode geometry for
n=3

In the
n=3
case, the effective action from Eq. (
5.84
) has the form

S_{\text{eff}}=3S_{I}-12S_{I}\left(e^{-\alpha_{1}}+e^{-\alpha_{2}}+e^{-\alpha_{3}}\right),\qquad\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=T\,.

(5.220)

Here
\alpha_{p}
are the distances between successive instanton centers. This action has an extremum with
\alpha_{p}=T/3
for all
p
. This is the real
n=3
saddle around which
S_{\text{eff}}
is locally valid. It is useful to introduce two coordinates
u
and
v
via

\alpha_{1}=\frac{T}{3}-u,\qquad\alpha_{2}=\frac{T}{3}-v,\qquad\alpha_{3}=\frac{T}{3}+u+v\,.

(5.221)

The
u
and
v
coordinates correspond to the collapse of the first or second instanton–anti-instanton pairs:

+1
-1
\alpha_{3}
\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{3}
u
u
v
v

(5.222)

Collapsing the third pair corresponds to
\alpha_{3}\to 0
, i.e.
u+v\to-T/3
, which is not an independent coordinate. In the
(u,v)
coordinates, the effective action is

S_{\text{eff}}(u,v)=3S_{I}-12S_{I}e^{-T/3}\left(e^{u}+e^{v}+e^{-u-v}\right)\,.

(5.223)

Following the convention of Eq. (
5.277
), the deviation from the saddle action is

S_{\text{eff}}^{(3)}(u,v)\equiv S_{\text{eff}}(u,v)-3S_{I}=-12S_{I}e^{-T/3}\left(e^{u}+e^{v}+e^{-u-v}\right)\,.

(5.224)

This action is shown in Figure
17
. The
n=3
saddle is the zenith of the plateau at
S_{\text{eff}}(u,v)\approx 3S_{I}
. The triangular structure of the simplex
\{\alpha_{p}>0;\penalty\ \sum\alpha_{p}=T\}
is evident in the symmetry of the action surface. When any of the three possible adjacent instanton pairs collapses, the same
n=1
saddle results. Although
S_{\text{eff}}
is not accurate near the
n=1
saddle (just as the
n=2
version of
S_{\text{eff}}
did not describe the
n=0
saddle in Section
5.5
), the saddle’s action is
S_{I}
, shown as a horizontal plane in the figure. The boundary edges of the simplex, where one
\alpha_{p}
saturates
\ln 6
, lie on this plane and are drawn as jagged lines in the right panel.

\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}^{(3)}=

\alpha_{3}\approx 0
\alpha_{2}\approx 0
\alpha_{1}\approx 0
n=3
saddle
u
v

Figure 17:
The
n=3
collective-coordinate geometry. On the left
the effective action
S_{\text{eff}}(u,v)
is shown. This action is valid near the
n=3
saddle, where
\alpha_{p}=T/3
and
u=v=0
, and descends to the
n=1
saddle where
S=S_{I}
, approximated by the red plane. The right panel shows the real
u,v
domain with the
n=3
saddle at the center. The triangle edges at
\alpha_{p}\approx\ln 6
represent the
n=1
instanton. The jagged lines indicate that the
n=1
saddle of the full action is not precisely described by
S_{\text{eff}}
on these edges.

The Picard–Lefschetz decomposition of
\widetilde{Z}
involves the thimble
\mathcal{J}_{1}
through the
n=1
saddle which contributes to
\widetilde{Z}_{1}
and the thimble
\mathcal{J}_{3}
through the
n=3
saddle, which contributes to
\widetilde{Z}_{3}
. The
\mathcal{J}_{1}
thimble departs from the
n=1
saddle and hits the Stokes point at the
n=3
saddle where it becomes complex and lines up with
\mathcal{J}_{3}
but with opposite orientation. The imaginary parts of the
\mathcal{J}_{1}
and
\mathcal{J}_{3}
thimbles cancel because they come from an identical cycle with opposite orientation. The geometric picture makes the cancellation trivial, as it did in
n=2
. To determine the corresponding parts of the trans-series we have to do the relevant integrals.

5.6.2
n=3
thimble integral

We begin by computing the integral
\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}
along
\mathcal{J}_{3}^{\pm}
, where
\pm
refer to the sign of the imaginary part in the lateral deformation:

\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}(T)=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{3}^{\pm}}du\,dv\,e^{-S_{\text{eff}}^{(3)}/\hbar}=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{3}^{\pm}}du\,dv\,\exp\!\left[\kappa_{3}\left(e^{u}+e^{v}+e^{-u-v}\right)\right],

(5.225)

where

\kappa_{3}\equiv\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/3}.

(5.226)

The contour
\mathcal{J}_{3}
is a middle-dimensional cycle in
{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
attached to the real saddle at
u=v=0
and ending in convergent asymptotic regions. To evaluate the integral, it is useful to start from same integral over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with the sign in the exponent flipped

\mathcal{I}^{\mathbb{R}}_{3}(\mu)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\,dy\,\exp\!\left[-\mu\left(e^{x}+e^{y}+e^{-x-y}\right)\right],\qquad\operatorname{Re}\mu>0,

(5.227)

which gives

\mathcal{I}^{\mathbb{R}}_{3}(\mu)=\mathcal{G}_{3}(\mu^{3}),

(5.228)

with
\mathcal{G}_{3}
a Meijer G-function defined in Eq. (
5.183
). The physical saddle has the opposite sign in the exponent, so it is obtained by continuing the real cycle to the negative
\mu
axis. During this continuation the real cycle deforms into the thimble
\mathcal{J}_{3}^{\pm}
, giving

\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}(T,\hbar)=\mathcal{I}^{\mathbb{R}}_{3}(\kappa_{3}e^{\pm i\pi})=\mathcal{G}_{3}(\kappa_{3}^{3}e^{\pm 3\pi i}).

(5.229)

Here
\kappa_{3}^{3}e^{\pm 3\pi i}
records the sheet reached by the lateral continuation of the real convergent integral. This is the direct two-dimensional replacement of the factor
\mathcal{G}_{2}(\kappa_{2}^{2}e^{\pm 2\pi i})=2K_{0}(2\kappa_{2}e^{\pm\pi i})
that appeared in the
n=2
thimble integral, Eq. (
5.185
).

To expand at large
T
, where
\kappa_{3}\to 0
, we can use

\mathcal{G}_{3}(z)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\log z+3\gamma_{E}\right)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(z\log^{2}z\right).

(5.230)

We then get

\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}=\frac{9}{2}T_{3}^{2}-\frac{17\pi^{2}}{4}\pm 9\pi i\,T_{3}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(T^{3}e^{-T}\right)

(5.231)

where

T_{3}=\log\kappa_{3}+\gamma_{E}=-\frac{T}{3}+\log\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)+\gamma_{E}\,.

(5.232)

This combination
T_{3}
appearing in
\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}
is nearly identical to the combination appearing in
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{+}
in Eq. (
5.181
): the
T
comes from the length of the plateau and the
\ln\hbar
from its exponentially small curvature.
The imaginary part of the
n=3
thimble integral is then

\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}=\pm\,9\pi\,T_{3}\,.

(5.233)

Combining with the collective-coordinate measure and the transverse determinant in Eq. (
5.146
), the trans-series coefficient is

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}_{3}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{2T}{3}\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\left(\frac{S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{3/2}\left(\frac{\det{\mathcal{O}}_{0}}{\det_{\perp}{\mathcal{O}}_{3}}\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}

(5.234)

\displaystyle=\frac{2T}{3}\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{3/2}\left[\frac{9}{2}T_{3}^{2}-\frac{17\pi^{2}}{4}\pm 9\pi i\,T_{3}\right]

(5.235)

The factor of
2
comes from the two parity-related three-instanton configurations. The factor of
1/3
multiplying
\widetilde{Z}_{3}^{\pm}
is the CC-fix factor, the correction to the standard collective-coordinate measure. We emphasize that the change of variables from local fluctuation coordinates to the collective coordinate
t_{0}
is in fact multi-valued, with the number of preimages
N_{\psi_{0}}[x]
equal to the number of barrier crossings of the path. The correct measure contains a compensating factor
1/N_{\psi_{0}}[x]

[
23
]
. For an
n
-instanton path
N_{\psi_{0}}=n
at leading order, so the CC-fix factor is
1/n
. For
n=2
in Eq. (
5.187
) the CC-fix factor canceled in
Z_{2}
against the factor of 2 coming from the two-well multiplicity.

5.6.3
n=1
asymptotic series

Around the
n=1
saddle, the 1-loop path integral and its large-
T
behavior were given in Eqs. (
5.138
) and (
5.139
). Stripping the
e^{-S_{I}/\hbar}
factor in accordance with the trans-series definition
\widetilde{Z}=\lambda\widetilde{Z}_{1}+\lambda^{3}\widetilde{Z}_{3}+\cdots
and including both orientations,

\widetilde{Z}_{1}=2Z_{\text{SHO}}\,T\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{1/2}+\cdots,

(5.236)

which is real at all orders in the perturbative expansion. The leading asymptotic growth of the
n=1
coefficient is governed by the nearest twisted saddle at
n=3
. As in the
n=2
analysis of
I_{0\to 2}^{\text{asy}}
, this growth is generated by the integral over quasi-collective coordinates. We first write the integral over the real simplex as

\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}=\int_{\Gamma_{\triangle}}du\,dv\,e^{-S_{\text{eff}}/\hbar}=e^{-3S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{\Gamma_{\triangle}}du\,dv\,\exp\!\left[\kappa_{3}\left(e^{u}+e^{v}+e^{-u-v}\right)\right]\,.

(5.237)

This is the
n=3
analog of the integral
I_{0\to 2}
over
\alpha
in the
n=2
case (Eq. (
5.191
)), with the simplex
\Gamma_{\triangle}
playing the role of the real segment
[\alpha_{0},T/2]
. The three sides of the triangle correspond to the three adjacent-pair collapse channels and are equivalent under cyclic relabeling, so we can pick the sector
\alpha_{1}\leq\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
(i.e.
-2u\leq v\leq u
) and multiply by
3
. Substituting
w=v+u/2
symmetrizes the inner range to
w\in[-3u/2,3u/2]
and brings the action into
\cosh
form,

\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}=3e^{-3S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{0}^{u_{0}}du\,e^{\kappa_{3}e^{u}}\int_{-3u/2}^{3u/2}dw\,e^{2\kappa_{3}e^{-u/2}\cosh w}\,.

(5.238)

It is natural to take
u_{0}=\frac{T}{3}-\log 6

in direct analogy with
\alpha_{0}=\log 6
in Eq. (
5.201
). With this choice
S_{\text{eff}}
spans the full
2S_{I}
action gap between the
n=1
and
n=3
saddles at leading order in
T
(
S_{\text{eff}}\to S_{I}
at
u=u_{0}
and
S_{\text{eff}}\to 3S_{I}
at
u=0
), matching the range of the full action between those saddles. As in the
n=2
case, this fixes the location of the Borel singularity, leaving only matching constants from the small-
\alpha
region which do not affect the leading asymptotic behavior.

The integral in Eq. (
5.238
) is over the whole triangle in Fig.
17
. It contains the directions of descent into the
n=1
saddle in the 3 directions (the triangle edges). However, it also includes regions where a second separation becomes small (the triangle corners). These corners are problematic: they are well outside of the validity of
S_{\text{eff}}
, with action well below
S_{I}
, and are not relevant to the asymptotic growth of
\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}
. To isolate the asymptotic growth of the
n=1
series we should sum the 3 collapse channels independently, where in each channel the third instanton is a spectator. The action in Eq. (
5.223
) has three terms,
e^{u}
,
e^{v}
and
e^{-u-v}
, corresponding to the three collapse channels, so to isolate one channel we can keep one of these terms at a time, treating the other two as constant. Focusing on the
u
channel, we keep
e^{u}
and drop the other two terms:
e^{v}\to 0
and
e^{-u-v}\to 0
. This amounts to setting
e^{2\kappa_{3}e^{-u/2}\cosh w}\to 1
in Eq. (
5.238
). Equivalently, we drop the
w
dependence so that
w
becomes a collective coordinate. We still need the volume of
w
, with the
\pm 3u/2
integration boundaries, like we would need the volume of an honest collective coordinate such as
t_{0}
. Thus we expect the leading asymptotic growth to be given by

\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}=3e^{-3S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{0}^{u_{0}}du\,e^{\kappa_{3}e^{u}}\int_{-3u/2}^{3u/2}dw+\cdots\,,

(5.239)

where the
\cdots
represent terms not relevant to the leading asymptotic growth of
\widetilde{Z}_{1}
.

Changing variables to
\xi=\kappa_{3}e^{u}
gives

\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}=9e^{-3S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{\kappa_{3}}^{\xi_{0}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\,e^{\xi}\log(\xi/\kappa_{3})+\cdots,\qquad\xi_{0}=\kappa_{3}e^{u_{0}}=\frac{2S_{I}}{\hbar}\,.

(5.240)

This integral is dominated by its upper endpoint region, near the
n=1
saddle at
\xi=\xi_{0}
where the integrand is exponentially large. Near the upper endpoint we split

\log(\xi/\kappa_{3})=\log(\xi_{0}/\kappa_{3})+\log(\xi/\xi_{0})\,.

(5.241)

The first term is

\log(\xi_{0}/\kappa_{3})=-T_{3}+\bigl(\log\xi_{0}+\gamma_{E}\bigr)\,,

(5.242)

which gives the dominant behavior of the integral, growing linearly with
T
. The second term in Eq. (
5.241
) vanishes at the upper endpoint and can only contribute at order
T^{0}
or lower. Retaining the part proportional to
T_{3}
, and noting that the resulting integral is the same as in the
n=2
case, Eq. (
5.192
), we then get

\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}=-9T_{3}\,e^{-3S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{\kappa_{3}}^{\xi_{0}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\,e^{\xi}+\cdots=+9T_{3}\,e^{-3S_{I}/\hbar}\operatorname{Ein}(-\xi_{0})+\cdots\,,

(5.243)

where in the last step we used the identity from Eq. (
5.192
),
\int_{a}^{b}du\,e^{u}/u=\operatorname{Ein}(-a)-\operatorname{Ein}(-b)+\ln(b/a)
, with
\operatorname{Ein}(-\kappa_{3})\to 0
as
\kappa_{3}\to 0
and the resulting
\ln(\xi_{0}/\kappa_{3})
piece absorbed into the
\cdots
. Since this factorial tail expression is obtained from integrating over the quasi-zero modes about the
n=3
saddle, we must also account for an extra
1/3
factor from the CC-fix
14
14

14

Note that in Eq.(
5.200
) the CC-fix factor from the
n=2
quasi-zero mode sector was implicit. There we had integrated along the single descent direction from
\alpha\in[\alpha_{0},T/2]
, which corresponds to integrating along a single face of the relevant simplex. Therefore the CC-fix factor of
1/2
canceled against the multiplicative factor of 2 associated with the 2 distinct collapse channels (or faces of the 1-simplex).
. Therefore, using
\xi_{0}=2S_{I}/\hbar
, and the trans-series expansion of
\operatorname{Ein}
in Eq. (
5.195
) we arrive at

\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}^{\rm asy}=-3T_{3}\,e^{-S_{I}/\hbar}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{n+1}+\cdots\,.

(5.244)

The omitted
\cdots
terms are not relevant to the leading asymptotic growth.

5.6.4
Resurgent structure at order
\lambda^{3}

To convert
\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}^{\text{asy}}
into the twisted partition function trans-series, we need to add the transverse determinant. Since
\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}^{\text{asy}}
was computed using the quasi-zero mode coordinates from around the
n=3
saddle, the relevant transverse determinant is over coordinates transverse to this space,
(\det{\mathcal{O}}_{0}/\det_{\perp}{\mathcal{O}}_{3})^{1/2}=12^{3/2}
. Combining the leading
\widetilde{Z}_{1}
from Eq. (
5.236
) with the factorial tail in Eq. (
5.244
), weighted by the determinant ratio, and accounting for the
\lambda
factor in the definition of
\widetilde{Z}_{1}
we get:

\widetilde{Z}_{1}=2T\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{1/2}\!\left[1+\cdots\,-\,3T_{3}\,\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n!\!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{n+1}\right]\,.

(5.245)

The lateral Borel resummation of this series gives

\mathcal{S}_{\pm}\bigl[\widetilde{Z}_{1}\bigr]=6TT_{3}\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{3/2}\!e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}E_{1}\!\left(-\frac{2S_{I}}{\hbar}\pm i\varepsilon\right)\,.

(5.246)

With
\operatorname{Im}E_{1}(-2S_{I}/\hbar\pm i\varepsilon)=\mp\pi
, the imaginary part is

\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{S}_{\pm}\bigl[\widetilde{Z}_{1}\bigr]=\mp\,6\pi TT_{3}\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{3/2}\!e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\,.

(5.247)

This exactly cancels the imaginary part from the
n=3
sector of the trans-series in Eq. (
5.235
). More precisely

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[\widetilde{Z}]=\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[e^{-S_{I}/\hbar}\widetilde{Z}_{1}]+\operatorname{Im}[e^{-3S_{I}/\hbar}\widetilde{Z}_{3}]+\cdots=0

(5.248)

establishing the reality of the Borel-resummed twisted partition function to order
\lambda^{3}
. This is the
n=3
counterpart of the
\lambda^{2}
cancellation in
Z
in Eq. (
5.211
) which we have verified in this subsection to leading order in
T_{3}
.

5.7
n=4
thimble decomposition

The geometric thimble picture of the quasi-zero-mode space is similar for any
n
. At
n=4
there is a new feature: the imaginary part of the integral along the
n=4
thimble is not completely canceled by the imaginary parts from thimbles associated with the nearest lower saddle at
n=2
. Instead, a thimble passing through
n=0
is also needed. Since the effective action
S_{\text{eff}}(\alpha_{p})
we have been using is derived at large
T
, it will not describe these distant saddles well enough to establish the complete cancellation. We can nevertheless see the cancellation geometrically and verify the cancellation of the leading component of the imaginary part at large
T
. We next discuss this new feature of the
n=4
case and then briefly summarize the resurgent structure for general
n
.

5.7.1
Quasi-zero-mode geometry for
n=4

At
n=4
we are back to the untwisted partition function. This case illustrates a new feature: there are 3 saddles, with
n=0,2,4
, all of which are relevant to the Picard–Lefschetz decomposition and the resurgent structure at order
\lambda^{4}
. For
n=4
, the effective action from Eq. (
5.84
) has the form

S_{\text{eff}}=4S_{I}-12S_{I}\left[e^{-\alpha_{1}}+e^{-\alpha_{2}}+e^{-\alpha_{3}}+e^{-\alpha_{4}}\right],\qquad\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}=T\,.

(5.249)

Here
\alpha_{p}
are the distances between successive instanton centers. The simplex
\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}^{4}=\{\alpha_{p}>0;\penalty\ \sum\alpha_{p}=T\}
is a tetrahedron in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, generalizing the triangle from Figure
17
. Imposing the constraint
\sum\alpha_{p}=T
with a Lagrange multiplier and extremizing the action leads to
\alpha_{p}^{\star}=T/4
for all
p
. This is the real
n=4
saddle around whose local neighborhood
S_{\text{eff}}
is valid.

We can diagonalize the Hessian with normal coordinates
t_{0},x_{1},x_{2},y
as

\alpha_{1}=\frac{T}{4}-x_{1}-y,\quad\alpha_{2}=\frac{T}{4}-x_{2}+y,\quad\alpha_{3}=\frac{T}{4}+x_{1}-y,\quad\alpha_{4}=\frac{T}{4}+x_{2}+y\,.

(5.250)

so that

\displaystyle S_{\text{eff}}

\displaystyle=4S_{I}-12S_{I}e^{-\frac{T}{4}}\left[e^{x_{1}+y}+e^{x_{2}-y}+e^{-x_{1}+y}+e^{-x_{2}-y}\right]

(5.251)

\displaystyle=4S_{I}-48S_{I}e^{-\frac{T}{4}}-12S_{I}e^{-\frac{T}{4}}(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+2y^{2})+\cdots

(5.252)

There is no dependence on the exact zero mode coordinate
t_{0}
.
The three normal modes act on the instanton separations as follows:

+1
-1
\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{3}
x_{1}
x_{1}
x_{2}
x_{2}
y
y
y
y

(5.253)

The
x_{1}
normal mode compresses
\alpha_{1}
and stretches
\alpha_{3}
, while
x_{2}
compresses
\alpha_{2}
and stretches
\alpha_{4}
. Each corresponds to a single instanton–anti-instanton pair collapsing. For example, as
x_{1}\to T/4
,
\alpha_{1}\to 0
so that the first and second instantons annihilate. The surviving pair then sits at separation
\alpha_{3}=T/2
, which is the
n=2
saddle of the reduced two-instanton problem on the same circle. The
y
mode is a breathing mode that compresses
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}
while stretching
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4}
. As
y\to T/4
both
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}\to 0
and the configuration degenerates to
n=0
.

The action
S_{\text{eff}}(x_{1},x_{2},y)
is only a local expression near the
n=4
saddle, which is where
x_{1},x_{2}
and
y
are sensible normal modes. The corresponding directions can nevertheless be followed in the full path space by gradient flow, as shown in Fig.
18
. The
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
directions first approach the
n=2
boundary sector, while the
y
direction directly approaches the perturbative
n=0
sector. The plateau near
2S_{I}
is therefore not a feature of the local
S_{\text{eff}}
chart; it is a feature of the full action along the flow. The natural QZM manifold (including the zero mode) has dimension
n
near the
n
-instanton saddle,

n=0:\ 0,\qquad n=2:\ (t_{0},\alpha),\qquad n=4:\ (t_{0},x_{1},x_{2},y).

(5.254)

Thus the lower sectors should be viewed as boundary levels of the compactified quasi-zero-mode space, not as ordinary interior critical points of the same local
n=4
coordinate action.

Figure 18:
Downward-flow trajectories from the
n=4
saddle along the three unstable normal-mode directions
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
y
at
T=40
. The top row shows the normalized action
S/S_{I}
along the flow coordinate, and the bottom row shows representative profiles
x(t)
(darker curves are earlier in the flow). The
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
modes each annihilate a single instanton–anti-instanton pair, reducing the action from
4S_{I}
to
\approx 2S_{I}
; the surviving pair then contracts and annihilates, reaching
S\approx 0
. The action plateaus near
2S_{I}
because any well-separated kink pair has
S\approx 2S_{I}
regardless of separation. That the kink separation at the plateau appears closer to
T/4
than
T/2
is an artifact of the flow integration: the QZM eigenvalues scale as
e^{-T/4}
(so
\sim e^{-10}
at
T=40
), making the time to escape the saddle exponentially sensitive to the size of the initial perturbation along
\Psi_{1}
.

For the
n=4
thimble integral, we follow the same strategy as in the
n=3
analysis: rather than work in the diagonal coordinates
(x_{1},x_{2},y)
of Eq. (
5.250
), which are local normal modes of the Hessian but mix the four collapse channels, we use simplex coordinates that resolve each adjacent-pair collapse on its own face. Following the general-
n
setup of Section
5.8
, we set

\alpha_{p}=\frac{T}{4}-u_{p}\quad(p=1,2,3),\qquad\alpha_{4}=\frac{T}{4}+u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3},

(5.255)

in which the effective action reads

S_{\text{eff}}(u)=4S_{I}-12S_{I}e^{-T/4}\Bigl[e^{u_{1}}+e^{u_{2}}+e^{u_{3}}+e^{-u_{1}-u_{2}-u_{3}}\Bigr]\,,

(5.256)

with the deviation from the saddle action

S_{\text{eff}}^{(4)}(u)\equiv S_{\text{eff}}(u)-4S_{I}=-12S_{I}e^{-T/4}\Bigl[e^{u_{1}}+e^{u_{2}}+e^{u_{3}}+e^{-u_{1}-u_{2}-u_{3}}\Bigr]\,.

(5.257)

The collective-coordinate domain
\Gamma_{\triangle}^{4}
is a tetrahedral 3-simplex, with four codimension-1 faces
\alpha_{p}\to 0
each describing one adjacent pair collapsing down to a
n=2
configuration. The Picard–Lefschetz decomposition pairs the saddle thimble
\mathcal{J}_{4}
through the real
n=4
saddle at
u=0
with boundary thimbles attached to the lower-
n
saddles, with imaginary parts canceling channel by channel as in the
n=3
case.

5.7.2
n=4
thimble integral

We first compute the integral along the thimble through the real
n=4
saddle. This integral is almost identical
to Eq. (
5.225
) and is evaluated the same way:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\pm}_{4}(T)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{4}^{\pm}}du_{1}\,du_{2}\,du_{3}\,e^{-S_{\text{eff}}^{(4)}/\hbar}=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{4}^{\pm}}du_{1}\,du_{2}\,du_{3}\,\exp\!\left[\kappa_{4}\Bigl(e^{u_{1}}+e^{u_{2}}+e^{u_{3}}+e^{-u_{1}-u_{2}-u_{3}}\Bigr)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{4}(\kappa_{4}^{4}e^{\pm 4\pi i})\,,

(5.258)

where
\kappa_{4}\equiv\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/4}
. The result is the
n=4
analog of Eq. (
5.229
). At large
T
,
\kappa_{4}\to 0
, and
\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}
expands to a polynomial in
T
(cf. Eq. (
5.293
) below):

\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}=-\frac{32}{3}T_{4}^{3}+\frac{92\pi^{2}}{3}T_{4}-\frac{4\zeta(3)}{3}\pm i\!\left(-32\pi T_{4}^{2}+\frac{28\pi^{3}}{3}\right)+\mathcal{O}\bigl(T^{4}e^{-T}\bigr)\,,

(5.259)

where

T_{4}\equiv\log\kappa_{4}+\gamma_{E}=\log\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)-\frac{T}{4}+\gamma_{E}\,.

(5.260)

Therefore

\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}=\mp 32\pi\,T_{4}^{2}\pm\frac{28\pi^{3}}{3}+\mathcal{O}(T^{4}e^{-T})\,.

(5.261)

Compared to
\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}=\pm 9\pi T_{3}
, the leading logarithmic degree is now quadratic instead of linear. Combining
\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}
with the collective-coordinate measure, the transverse determinant from Eq. (
5.146
), the two-well multiplicity factor of
2
, and the CC-fix factor
1/4
for the 4-instanton paths, gives

\displaystyle Z_{4}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{4}\,T\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\left(\frac{S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\det{\mathcal{O}}_{0}}{\det_{\perp}{\mathcal{O}}_{4}}\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}

(5.262)

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{2}\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{2}\left[-\frac{32}{3}T_{4}^{3}+\frac{92\pi^{2}}{3}T_{4}-\frac{4\zeta(3)}{3}\pm i\!\left(-32\pi T_{4}^{2}+\frac{28\pi^{3}}{3}\right)\right]\,.

(5.263)

Here the prefactor
2/4=2/N_{\psi_{0}}
combines the two-well multiplicity factor with the CC-fix factor
1/N_{\psi_{0}}
.

5.7.3
n=2
asymptotic series

Next, we compute the leading asymptotic series around the
n=2
saddle, following Section
5.6.3
closely. The full real-cycle integral in the
n=4
quasi-zero-mode space is

\mathcal{I}_{\triangle}^{4}=e^{-4S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{\Gamma_{\triangle}^{4}}du_{1}\,du_{2}\,du_{3}\,\exp\!\left[\kappa_{4}\Bigl(e^{u_{1}}+e^{u_{2}}+e^{u_{3}}+e^{-u_{1}-u_{2}-u_{3}}\Bigr)\right]\,.

(5.264)

This is the analog of
\mathcal{I}_{1\to 3}
in Eq. (
5.237
) but now the integration cycle
\Gamma_{\triangle}^{4}
is over a tetrahedron instead of a triangle. The center of the tetrahedron is the
n=4
saddle, and the directions from the center to a face along each
u_{p}
correspond to the annihilation of an adjacent instanton-anti-instanton pair. Analogous to the
n=3
case, the dominant asymptotic growth of the
n=2
trans-series comes from integrations along a single quasi-zero mode coordinate
u_{p}
at a time with the others treated as static and well separated. So, labeling
u
the coordinate to be integrated, and
w_{1},w_{2}
the static ones, we have

\mathcal{I}_{2\to 4}^{\text{asy}}=4e^{-4S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{0}^{u_{0}}du\,e^{\kappa_{4}e^{u}}\int_{\Gamma_{2}(u)}dw_{1}dw_{2}+\cdots,

(5.265)

where
u_{0}=T/4-\log 6
and, for the face where
\alpha_{1}
collapses,

\Gamma_{2}(u)=\{(w_{1},w_{2}):\;w_{1}\leq u,\;w_{2}\leq u,\;w_{1}+w_{2}\geq-2u\}.

(5.266)

The area of this triangle is
8u^{2}
. Therefore

\mathcal{I}_{2\to 4}^{\text{asy}}=32e^{-4S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{0}^{u_{0}}du\,u^{2}e^{\kappa_{4}e^{u}}+\cdots=32e^{-4S_{I}/\hbar}\int_{\kappa_{4}}^{\xi_{0}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\,e^{\xi}\log^{2}(\xi/\kappa_{4})+\cdots,

(5.267)

with
\xi_{0}=\kappa_{4}e^{u_{0}}=2S_{I}/\hbar
. Expanding around the upper endpoint as in the
n=3
case and accounting for the CC-fix factor of
1/4
gives the relevant asymptotic expansion as

\mathcal{I}_{2\to 4}^{\text{asy}}=8\,T_{4}^{2}\,e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{n+1}+\cdots\,.

(5.268)

This is the piece whose lateral Borel ambiguity cancels the leading
T_{4}^{2}
piece of
\operatorname{Im}Z_{4}^{\pm}
. The omitted terms are of order
\mathcal{O}(T_{4})
and are sensitive to how the boundary of the simplex is handled.

Combining Eq. (
5.268
) with the leading
n=2
sector, weighted by the determinant ratio, including the parity factor of
2
from the two-well multiplicity (just as for
Z_{4}
above), and removing the
e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}
factor in accordance with the trans-series definition
Z=Z_{0}+\lambda^{2}Z_{2}+\lambda^{4}Z_{4}+\cdots
, gives

Z_{2}=TZ_{\text{SHO}}\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)\left[1+\cdots+16\,T_{4}^{2}\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{n+1}\right].

(5.269)

The factorial-tail coefficient is
16=8\times 2
, with the
2
from the two-well multiplicity applied when forming the
Z_{2}
trans-series coefficient. The lateral resummation of this tail gives

\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[Z_{2}]=TZ_{\text{SHO}}\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)\left[1+\cdots-16\,T_{4}^{2}\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}E_{1}\!\left(-\frac{2S_{I}}{\hbar}\pm i\varepsilon\right)\right],

(5.270)

so that

\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[Z_{2}]=\pm\,TZ_{\text{SHO}}\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{2}e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\,16\pi T_{4}^{2}\,.

(5.271)

In the full trans-series, the order-
\lambda^{4}
imaginary contribution is
\lambda^{2}\,\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[Z_{2}]+\lambda^{4}\,\operatorname{Im}Z_{4}=e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\,\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[Z_{2}]+e^{-4S_{I}/\hbar}\,\operatorname{Im}Z_{4}
. Both pieces carry the same
e^{-4S_{I}/\hbar}
prefactor, and comparing with Eq. (
5.263
) we see that the leading
T_{4}^{2}
ambiguity of
\lambda^{2}\,\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[Z_{2}]
cancels that of
\lambda^{4}Z_{4}
.

This is the partition-function analog of the alien-calculus cancellation at
\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{4})
derived in Eq. (
4.170
):

\operatorname{Im}[E_{4}]+\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{Im}[\Delta_{2}E_{2}]+\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{Im}[\Delta_{4}E_{P}]=0\,.

(5.272)

The partition-function version reads

\underbrace{\operatorname{Im}[Z_{4}]}_{TT_{4}^{2}+T+\cdots}\;+\;\tfrac{1}{2}\underbrace{\operatorname{Im}[\Delta_{2}Z_{2}]}_{TT_{4}^{2}+T+\cdots}\;+\;\tfrac{1}{2}\underbrace{\operatorname{Im}[\Delta_{4}Z_{0}]}_{T+\cdots}\;=\;0\,,

(5.273)

where the underbraces show the leading
T_{4}
-degrees in falling powers. At the highest power
TT_{4}^{2}
, only
\operatorname{Im}[Z_{4}]
and
\operatorname{Im}[\Delta_{2}Z_{2}]
contribute — these are precisely what we have computed (the
T_{4}^{2}
pieces of
\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}
and the leading collapse-face asymptotic of
\mathcal{I}_{2\to 4}
, respectively), and they cancel as discussed. The third piece
\operatorname{Im}[\Delta_{4}Z_{0}]
first enters at the subleading
T_{4}
level: it comes from the codimension-2 collapse face of the
n=4
simplex, where two instanton–anti-instanton pairs collapse simultaneously, and we have not computed it. The leftover
\pm 28\pi^{3}/3
in
\operatorname{Im}\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}
(Eq. (
5.261
)) is the
T_{4}^{0}
piece of
\operatorname{Im}[Z_{4}]
that awaits cancellation against
\operatorname{Im}[\Delta_{4}Z_{0}]
together with subleading-
T_{4}^{0}
pieces of
\operatorname{Im}[\Delta_{2}Z_{2}]
.

Note that the full imaginary part is not canceled with what we have computed so far. The remaining part of order
T_{4}^{0}
must be canceled by a combination of the subleading terms in the
n=2
sector and the
n=0
contribution. This cancellation is guaranteed by the Picard–Lefschetz decomposition of
S_{\text{eff}}
, and can in principle be verified. That is, the
T_{4}^{0}
pieces computed from
S_{\text{eff}}
must close the cancellation among
S_{\text{eff}}
-based quantities (
\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}
,
\mathcal{I}_{2\to 4}
,
\mathcal{I}_{0\to 2}
) by construction, since the integral over the tetrahedron is real. The real issue is that since
S_{\text{eff}}
is not approximating the full action well in the boundary-sensitive regions generating these
T_{4}^{0}
pieces, the resulting trans-series terms should not be expected to agree with terms in the actual trans-series of
Z
. The leading-
T_{4}
cancellation is the part that is robust to the
S_{\text{eff}}
approximation. In other words, closing the imaginary cancellation to order
T_{4}^{0}
would just be testing
S_{\text{eff}}
against itself and would not tell us anything new about the double well.

5.8
General
n

The structure exposed by the
n=2
,
n=3
, and
n=4
analyses generalizes cleanly to all
n
. At any instanton number
n
, the effective action from Eq. (
5.84
) reads

S_{\text{eff}}=nS_{I}-12S_{I}\sum_{p=1}^{n}e^{-\alpha_{p}},\qquad\sum_{p=1}^{n}\alpha_{p}=T\,,

(5.274)

with a real saddle at
\alpha_{p}=T/n
for every
p
. We can pick
n-1
local coordinates
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n-1}
that resolve the
n-1
adjacent-pair collapse channels, with a constraint that determines the last separation:

\alpha_{p}=\frac{T}{n}-u_{p}\quad(p=1,\ldots,n-1),\qquad\alpha_{n}=\frac{T}{n}+\sum_{p=1}^{n-1}u_{p}\,.

(5.275)

Each
u_{p}
collapses an adjacent pair when
u_{p}\to T/n
, while
\alpha_{n}\to 0
corresponds to
\sum_{p}u_{p}\to-T/n
and is not an independent direction. In these coordinates the action is

S_{\text{eff}}(u)=nS_{I}-12S_{I}e^{-T/n}\Bigl[\sum_{p=1}^{n-1}e^{u_{p}}+e^{-\sum_{p}u_{p}}\Bigr]\,.

(5.276)

It is convenient to subtract the saddle action
nS_{I}
from
S_{\text{eff}}
, defining the deviation

S_{\text{eff}}^{(n)}(u)\equiv S_{\text{eff}}(u)-nS_{I}=-12S_{I}e^{-T/n}\Bigl[\sum_{p=1}^{n-1}e^{u_{p}}+e^{-\sum_{p}u_{p}}\Bigr]\,,

(5.277)

so that
S_{\text{eff}}^{(n)}=0
at the saddle and the
e^{-nS_{I}/\hbar}
factor does not need to be carried through every formula in the QZM analysis.
The real integration domain
\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}^{(n)}=\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}:\ \alpha_{p}\geq 0\ \forall p\}
is the
(n-1)
-simplex, generalizing the triangle of the
n=3
case (Fig.
17
). Each of its
n
codimension-one faces
\alpha_{p}\to 0
is an adjacent-pair collapse channel down to the
n-2
saddle in the same parity sector, on which the effective action
S_{\text{eff}}
is no longer trustworthy and matching to the full action is required.

5.8.1
General-
n
thimble integral

The integral over
\mathcal{J}_{n}^{\pm}
is

\mathcal{I}^{\pm}_{n}(T)=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{n}^{\pm}}d^{n-1}u\,e^{-S_{\text{eff}}^{(n)}/\hbar}=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{n}^{\pm}}d^{n-1}u\,\exp\!\left[\kappa_{n}\Bigl(\sum_{p=1}^{n-1}e^{u_{p}}+e^{-\sum_{p}u_{p}}\Bigr)\right],

(5.278)

with

\kappa_{n}\equiv\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/n}\,.

(5.279)

and
\mathcal{J}_{n}^{\pm}
the middle-dimensional Lefschetz thimble through the saddle at
u_{p}=0
. To recover the thimble integral it is easier to first consider the real-cycle integral

\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mu)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}d^{n-1}\xi\,\exp\!\left[-\mu\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{a_{i}\cdot\xi}\right],\qquad\operatorname{Re}\mu>0\,,

(5.280)

where the
n
exponent vectors
a_{p}=\mathbf{e}_{p}
for
p=1,\ldots,n-1
and
a_{n}=-\sum_{p=1}^{n-1}\mathbf{e}_{p}
obey the linear relation
\sum_{i}a_{i}=0
. The collapse to a single-variable Meijer
G
-function comes from a Mellin–Barnes computation. First, we use the Cahen–Mellin integral representation

e^{-y}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{C}ds\,\Gamma(s)y^{-s}\,,

(5.281)

where
C
is the vertical line in the complex plane passing through
c>0
, to rewrite the integrand of Eq. (
5.280
) as

e^{-\mu\sum_{i}e^{a_{i}\cdot\xi}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{C^{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}ds_{i}\,\Gamma(s_{i})\,\mu^{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}}\,\exp\!\left[-\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}a_{i}\right)\!\cdot\xi\right]\,.

(5.282)

Choosing each
s_{i}
on the same vertical contour
\operatorname{Re}s_{i}=c
as
s_{i}=c+it_{i}
, the linear relation
\sum_{i}a_{i}=0
removes the real part of
\sum_{i}s_{i}a_{i}
and the
\xi
integral collapses to a Dirac delta:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}d^{n-1}\xi\,e^{-i(\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}a_{i})\cdot\xi}=(2\pi)^{n-1}\delta^{(n-1)}\!\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}a_{i}\right)\,.

(5.283)

The constraint
\sum_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}a_{i}=0
in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
pins down
t_{1}=t_{2}=\cdots=t_{n}\equiv t
, so that after taking the integrals over
s_{1},\cdots,s_{n-1}
, we are left with a single contour integral

\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mu)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{C}ds\,\Gamma(s)^{n}\mu^{-ns}=\mathcal{G}_{n}(\mu^{n})\,.

(5.284)

This is exactly the Mellin–Barnes representation of the all-zero-parameter Meijer
G
-function in Eq. (
5.183
), appearing in Eq. (
5.185
), Eq. (
5.228
) and Eq. (
5.258
) for
n=2,3,4
respectively. The physical thimble integral (
5.278
) has the opposite sign in the exponent and is obtained by analytic continuation
\mu\to\kappa_{n}e^{\pm i\pi}
. Continuous tracking of the rotation gives
\mu^{n}\to\kappa_{n}^{n}e^{\pm n\pi i}
and the thimble integral reads

\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}(T)=\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\mathbb{R}}\!\bigl(\kappa_{n}e^{\pm i\pi}\bigr)=\mathcal{G}_{n}\!\bigl(\kappa_{n}^{n}e^{\pm n\pi i}\bigr)\,.

(5.285)

which is the generalization of the cases for
n=2,3,4
.

To evaluate (
5.285
) at large
T
, we close the contour in the Mellin–Barnes integrand

\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}(T)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{C}ds\,\Gamma(s)^{n}\,\kappa_{n}^{-ns}\,e^{\mp in\pi s}

(5.286)

to the left. The poles of
\Gamma(s)^{n}
are at
s=0,-1,-2,\ldots
; the residue at
s=-k
carries a factor
\kappa_{n}^{nk}\propto e^{-kT}
and is exponentially suppressed at large
T
relative to the
s=0
residue. Keeping only the leading
s=0
residue,

\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}(T)=\mathop{\rm Res}_{s=0}\!\left[\Gamma(s)^{n}\,\kappa_{n}^{-ns}\,e^{\mp in\pi s}\right]+\mathcal{O}(e^{-T})\,,

(5.287)

where the factors of
i
from
1/(2\pi i)
and from the closed contour have cancelled. To extract the order-
n
residue, we write
\Gamma(s)^{n}=\Gamma(1+s)^{n}/s^{n}
and Taylor-expand using
\log\Gamma(1+s)=-\gamma_{E}s+\sum_{k\geq 2}(-s)^{k}\zeta(k)/k
:

\Gamma(1+s)^{n}\,\kappa_{n}^{-ns}=\exp\!\left[-nT_{n}\,s+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{n(-1)^{k}\zeta(k)}{k}\,s^{k}\right]=\sum_{m\geq 0}P_{m}^{(n)}(nT_{n})\,s^{m}\,,

(5.288)

where

T_{n}\equiv\log\kappa_{n}+\gamma_{E}=-\frac{T}{n}+\log\!\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)+\gamma_{E}\,,

(5.289)

The polynomials
P_{m}^{(n)}(u)
are defined by Eq. (
5.288
) and can be written explicitly as a sum over integer partitions of
m
:

P_{m}^{(n)}(u)=\sum_{q_{1}+2q_{2}+\cdots+mq_{m}=m}\frac{(-u)^{q_{1}}}{q_{1}!}\prod_{r=2}^{m}\frac{1}{q_{r}!}\left[\frac{n(-1)^{r}\zeta(r)}{r}\right]^{q_{r}}.

(5.290)

Each
P_{m}^{(n)}(u)
is a polynomial of degree
m
in
u
, with
n
-dependent coefficients. For example,

\displaystyle P_{0}^{(n)}(u)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle P_{1}^{(n)}(u)

\displaystyle=-u,

(5.291)

\displaystyle P_{2}^{(n)}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+\frac{n\pi^{2}}{12},

\displaystyle P_{3}^{(n)}(u)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}u^{3}-\frac{n\pi^{2}}{12}u-\frac{n\zeta(3)}{3}\,.

(5.292)

Combining with
e^{\mp in\pi s}=\sum_{j}(\mp in\pi)^{j}s^{j}/j!
, the residue at
s=0
is the coefficient of
s^{n-1}
in the product, which is then picked out by
m+j=n-1
. So,

\boxed{\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}(T)=\mathcal{G}_{n}\!\left[\kappa_{n}^{n}e^{\pm n\pi i}\right]=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}P_{n-1-j}^{(n)}(nT_{n})\,\frac{(\mp in\pi)^{j}}{j!}+\mathcal{O}(e^{-T})\,.}

(5.293)

This is the full
\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}
at large
T
, with both real and imaginary parts.

5.8.2
Resurgent structure at general
n

The polynomials
P_{m}^{(n)}(nT_{n})
are real, so the imaginary part comes from the odd-
j
terms, where
(\mp in\pi)^{j}=\mp i\,(-1)^{(j-1)/2}(n\pi)^{j}
:

\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}(T)=\mp\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\,\text{odd}\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\frac{(n\pi)^{j}}{j!}\,(-1)^{(j-1)/2}\;P_{n-1-j}^{(n)}\!\bigl(nT_{n}\bigr)\;+\;\mathcal{O}\!\bigl(\kappa_{n}^{n}T_{n}^{n-2}\bigr)\,.

(5.294)

This produces imaginary contributions at every degree
T_{n}^{n_{1}}\pi^{n_{2}}\prod_{k\geq 3}\zeta^{n_{k}}(k)
where
n_{2}\geq 1
is odd,
k\geq 3
is odd and
n_{1}+n_{2}+\sum_{k\geq 3}kn_{k}=n-1
. Evaluating (
5.294
) explicitly for the first few
n
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{2}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\mp\,2\pi,

(5.295)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{3}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\,9\pi\,T_{3},

(5.296)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{4}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\mp\,32\pi\,T_{4}^{2}\pm\frac{28\pi^{3}}{3},

(5.297)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{5}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\,\frac{625\pi}{6}\,T_{5}^{3}\mp\frac{375\pi^{3}}{4}\,T_{5}\pm\frac{25\pi\zeta(3)}{3},

(5.298)

recovering the results of the previous sections. Notably, the leading large
T
imaginary part is obtained by evaluating Eq. (
5.294
) at
j=1
. Making use of
P_{m}^{(n)}(nT_{n})=(-nT_{n})^{m}/m!+\mathcal{O}(T_{n}^{m-2})
we have
\operatorname{Im}\,\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}=\mp n\pi T^{n-2}/(n-2)!+\cdots
. Similarly, the leading large
T
real part is obtained from Eq. (
5.293
) by taking
m=n-1
and
j=0
to give
\operatorname{Re}\,\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}=(-nT_{n})^{n-1}/(n-1)!+\cdots=T^{n-1}/(n-1)!-n\bigl(\log(12S_{I}/\hbar)+\gamma_{E}\bigr)T^{n-2}/(n-2)!+\cdots
.

This leading imaginary part is canceled by the resummation of the
n-2
asymptotic series. In the
n
-instanton quasi-zero-mode space we calculate the real-cycle integral over the simplex
\Gamma_{\Delta}^{(n)}
as

\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}^{(n)}=\int_{\Gamma_{\Delta}^{(n)}}d^{n-1}u\,\exp\!\left[\kappa_{n}\left(\sum_{p=1}^{n-1}e^{u_{p}}+e^{-\sum_{p=1}^{n-1}u_{p}}\right)\right],\qquad\kappa_{n}=\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}e^{-T/n}\,.

(5.299)

The leading large-order growth of the
(n-2)
-instanton sector is controlled by the codimension-one faces of this simplex, where one single instanton–anti-instanton pair collapses. Generically picking the face where
\alpha_{1}
is the smallest separation and writing
u\equiv u_{1}
,
w_{a}\equiv u_{a+1}
,
a=1,\ldots,n-2
, the sector condition
\alpha_{1}\leq\alpha_{p}
gives

\Gamma_{n-2}(u)=\left\{w_{a}\leq u,\quad\sum_{a=1}^{n-2}w_{a}\geq-2u\right\}\,,

(5.300)

which has volume

\operatorname{Vol}\Gamma_{n-2}(u)=\frac{(nu)^{n-2}}{(n-2)!}\,.

(5.301)

Summing over the
n
equivalent collapse faces and including the CC-fix factor
1/n
, the leading face finite part is therefore

\mathcal{I}_{n-2\to n}^{\rm asy}=\frac{n^{n-2}}{(n-2)!}\int_{0}^{u_{\max}}du\,u^{n-2}e^{\kappa_{n}e^{u}}+\cdots,\qquad u_{\max}=\frac{T}{n}-\log 6\,.

(5.302)

After changing variables to
\xi=\kappa_{n}e^{u}
and retaining the part of the integral near the upper endpoint region to get the leading order in
T_{n}
, we get the asymptotic series

\mathcal{I}_{n-2\to n}^{\rm asy}=\frac{n^{n-2}}{(n-2)!}(-T_{n})^{n-2}\,e^{2S_{I}/\hbar}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\ell!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{\ell+1}+\cdots\,.

(5.303)

This is the piece whose lateral Borel resummation cancels the leading
T_{n}^{n-2}
imaginary part of
\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}
in Eq. (
5.294
) and demonstrates that for any instanton sector
n
, the asymptotic series of the
n-2
instanton sector is required to have a real and unambiguous partition function.

5.8.3
Partition function at general
n

Combining
\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}
with the collective-coordinate measure, the transverse determinant from Eq. (
5.146
), the two-well multiplicity factor of
2
, and the CC-fix factor
1/n
, the
n
-instanton partition function at NLO at large
T
becomes

Z_{n}^{\pm}=\frac{2}{n}\,Z_{\text{SHO}}\,T\,\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{n/2}\!\!\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm},

(5.304)

where the prefactor
2/n
combines the two-well multiplicity and CC-fix factors. The lateral imaginary part of this
n
-instanton trans-series is (
5.294
) dressed by the same prefactors.

Equivalently, the leading contribution from the
n
-instanton thimble and the leading asymptotic tail generated by the neighboring
(n+2)
-instanton saddle combine into the schematic large-
T
prediction

\displaystyle Z_{n}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=Z_{\text{SHO}}\,T\left(\frac{6S_{I}}{\pi\hbar}\right)^{n/2}\left[\frac{2\,T^{n-1}}{n!}-\frac{2\,T^{n-2}}{(n-2)!}\Big(\log\!\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}+\gamma_{E}\pm\pi i\Big)+\cdots\right.

(5.305)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{12S_{I}}{\pi\hbar}\,\frac{T^{n}}{n!}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\ell!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{\ell+1}+\cdots\right].

Summing over even
n
and using
Z_{\text{SHO}}=1/(2\sinh(T/2))
leads to

Z^{\pm}=\frac{\cosh(e^{-S_{I}/\hbar}KT)}{\sinh(T/2)}\left[1-TK^{2}e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\Big(\log\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}+\gamma_{E}\pm i\pi\Big)+TK^{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\ell!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2S_{I}}\right)^{\ell+1}+\cdots\right]

(5.306)

where
K=\sqrt{6S_{I}/(\pi\hbar)}
. The twisted partition function sums over odd
n
leading to an identical result with the replacement
\cosh\to\sinh
. The single
T
inside the bracket is from the center of the instanton–anti-instanton pair whose collapse leads to the subleading factors shown. The
\cosh/\sinh
prefactor is the result from the dilute instanton gas. The
\log\hbar
, imaginary part, and asymptotic series are all beyond the DIG.

The expression in Eq. (
5.306
) when expanded gives the trans-series for the partition function. Note however that not every term in this expansion comes from the saddle-point approximation: the imaginary part and
\ln\hbar
factors come from the full integral along the thimble in the quasi-zero-mode subspace. This was necessary because the quasi-zero modes have exponentially small (in
T
) eigenvalues so the saddle-point approximation in those directions is ill-defined as
T\to\infty
. Using Eq. (
5.195
), the imaginary parts cancel and the resummed result is manifestly real:

\mathcal{S}_{\pm}[Z_{\pm}]=\frac{\cosh(e^{-S_{I}/\hbar}KT)}{\sinh(T/2)}\left[1-TK^{2}e^{-2S_{I}/\hbar}\Big(\operatorname{Ein}(-\tfrac{2S_{I}}{\hbar})+\log 6\Big)+\cdots\right]\,,

(5.307)

where the constant
\log 6
arises from combining
\log(12S_{I}/\hbar)+\gamma_{E}
in Eq. (
5.306
) with the
\gamma_{E}+\log(2S_{I}/\hbar)
generated when the factorial tail is rewritten via Eq. (
5.195
). The cancellation is a rigorous large-
T
path-integral result, as illustrated for
\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{4})
by Eq. (
5.273
). Subleading-
T
cancellations involve higher alien derivatives (
\Delta_{4},\Delta_{6},\ldots
) acting on lower sectors and correspondingly higher-codimension collapse faces, neither of which we have computed; they are absorbed into the trailing
\cdots
.

Finally, we collect together everything we have computed so far. Specializing to real instantons (
k^{\prime}=0
,
n=2k
) and inserting into the master factorized expression for the partition function in Eq. (
5.106
) the formula for
\|\dot{x}_{k,0}\|^{2}=n\,S_{N}^{0}(\varepsilon)
from Eq. (
5.124
), the saddle action
S_{k,0}=n\,S_{N}^{0}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon T
from Eq. (
5.51
), the combined collective-coordinate Jacobian and transverse determinant factor
(12S_{I}/(2\pi\hbar))^{n/2}/(2\sinh(T/2))
at large
T
(with two-well multiplicity and CC-fix factor
2/n
, see Eq. (
5.146
)), and
\mathcal{I}_{n}^{\pm}
in
\mathcal{G}_{n}
form from Eq. (
5.285
), we get

\boxed{Z_{n}^{\pm}=\frac{2}{n}\sqrt{\frac{S_{N}^{0}(\varepsilon)}{S_{I}}}\,\frac{T}{2\sinh(T/2)}\,\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{2\pi\hbar}\right)^{n/2}\,e^{-\bigl(n\,S_{N}^{0}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon T\bigr)/\hbar}\,e^{T\Delta_{V}}\,e^{n\Delta_{L}}\,\mathcal{G}_{n}\left[\left(\frac{12S_{I}}{\hbar}\right)^{n}e^{-T\pm n\pi i}\right]}\,.

(5.308)

The saddle action is exact at finite
T
; the prefactor
\sqrt{S_{N}^{0}(\varepsilon)/S_{I}}
is the finite-
T
correction to the zero-mode Jacobian and equals
1
at large
T
where
S_{N}^{0}\to S_{I}
; the combined Jacobian and transverse-determinant factor
(12S_{I}/(2\pi\hbar))^{n/2}/(2\sinh(T/2))
is the standard large-
T
form, as discussed below Eq. (
5.146
). The expression is exact at finite
T
for the sectors with no QZMs (
n=0,1
, including via the
n=1
result Eq. (
5.138
)). For
n\geq 2
where the QZMs are present it drops terms exponentially suppressed in
T
. Although the QZM thimble integral factor is written in its Meijer
G
-function form, this should be understood only as its large-
T
expansion which reduces
\mathcal{G}_{n}[\cdots]
to the complex polynomial of degree
n-1
in Eq. (
5.293
). The perturbative vacuum-bubble
\Delta_{V}
and instanton-loop
\Delta_{L}
factors are in Eqs. (
5.163
) and (
5.169
